She initially poured herself into art‑therapy training plans, meticulous household budgeting, and her garden, but gradually her routines became less grounding and more rigid, and she withdrew from her book club and local arts events she once loved. Her family noticed that she stopped participating in shared activities, let household tasks lapse despite being at home, and spent hours isolated in the rose garden or at her laptop without finishing projects she had started, which led to growing tension and arguments with her spouse about her apparent loss of motivation and engagement. After an especially strained family conversation where she admitted that even painting and cooking no longer felt meaningful, her spouse encouraged her to see a psychiatrist to understand why she no longer cared about the activities and relationships that once defined her days.\n- Chief Complaint: “I don’t feel any interest in the things I used to love—my art, my garden, even time with my family just feels flat.”\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S019 (Delusions): I keep coming back to this idea that my husband’s work trips and the kids pulling away aren’t normal changes, but part of a plan to push me out of the family so they can watch how I react, and no matter how many times they tell me that’s not true, it still feels like there’s some test going on that I’m failing.\n- S020 (Hallucinations): Sometimes when I’m alone in the house, especially late at night, I hear a man and a woman whispering my name from the hallway or the garden, commenting on how I’m doing as a mother, even though I know there’s no one actually there.\n- S021 (Disorganized_Thinking_Speech): When I try to explain what’s going on in my head, I start talking about the garden, then jump to money, then to my kids’ future, and halfway through I forget what you even asked me because my thoughts just slide off in different directions.\n- S024 (Depressed_Mood): Most days I wake up with this heavy, sinking feeling, like there’s a gray film over everything, and even when nothing particularly bad is happening I just feel sad and used up for no clear reason.\n- S025 (Loss_of_Interest): I used to lose hours happily painting or planning out new plants for the yard, and now I just sit there staring at the canvas or the seed packets, feeling nothing and wondering why I should bother when it all seems pointless.\n- S026 (Weight_Appetite_Change): My eating has been all over the place—some weeks I barely feel hungry and skip meals without noticing, then other weeks I stand in the kitchen at night mindlessly snacking even though I’m not really tasting the food, and my clothes have started fitting differently because of it.\n- S030 (Worthlessness_Guilt): I walk around with this constant feeling that I’ve ruined my kids’ lives by checking out emotionally and that every argument or bad grade is somehow my fault, like I’m a burden they’d all be better off without.\n- S032 (Death_Suicide): On my worst days I catch myself thinking it wouldn’t be so bad if I just didn’t wake up, and I’ve gone as far as picturing driving my car into a tree, but then I get scared and push the thought away because I don’t actually want to do that to my family.\n- S033 (Elevated_Irritable_Mood): There are stretches where I suddenly feel wired and almost giddy for no good reason, talking fast and getting annoyed at everyone over tiny things, like the dishwasher not being loaded my way, and I can’t seem to dial it back.\n- S034 (Increased_Activity): When I get into those high-energy phases, I rush around the house starting a dozen projects—reorganizing closets, redesigning the garden layout, planning online art courses—all at once, and then crash later with half-finished piles everywhere.\n- S035 (Inflated_Self_Esteem): In those up periods, I get this strong conviction that I’m meant to create some huge, important art-therapy program that will change how people heal, and I feel almost embarrassed later when I look back at how certain I was.\n- S036 (Decreased_Sleep): During the more ‘up’ times I can go on three or four hours of sleep, spending half the night on the laptop planning things or rearranging the house, and I still wake up buzzing and not the least bit tired.\n- S038 (Racing_Thoughts): My mind can feel like a radio stuck between stations, with ideas about money, the kids, paint colors, and spiritual stuff all blasting at once so fast that I can’t grab onto any single thought long enough to finish it.\n- S039 (Manic_Distractibility): When I’m in that keyed-up mode, I’ll be paying bills and then a bird outside or a notification on my phone pulls me away, and before I know it I’m watering plants or looking up something random online, leaving everything half done.\n- S040 (Increased_Goal_Activity): In those higher-energy spells I start emailing old friends, signing up for workshops, planning big family outings, and even initiating sex more than usual, but I rarely follow through and end up feeling embarrassed and exhausted afterward.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- psychotic_symptoms_and_manic_episode_occur_concurrently: Some of the times when I’ve felt the most wired and slept only a few hours, racing around starting projects, were also when the whispers about my parenting were the loudest and I was most convinced my family was secretly testing me.\n- manic_episode_is_present_for_the_majority_of_the_total_duration_of_the_active_and_residual_portions_of_the_illness: Looking back over the last couple of years, those sped‑up, overconfident phases where I’m buzzing with ideas and starting big plans have taken up more of the time than the really low stretches, even if they come and go.\n- delusions_and_hallucinations_occur_for_2_or_more_weeks_in_the_absence_of_a_major_mood_episode: There was a stretch last year when my mood felt more or less neutral, but I still heard the nighttime whispering and was sure my husband’s trips were part of some experiment on me, even though I wasn’t particularly high or low emotionally.\n- functional_impairment: I’ve let bills pile up, stopped going to my book club and art events, and even at home I miss appointments and forget basic chores, so my relationships feel strained and I don’t feel capable of holding a job or finishing the projects I start.\n- duration: All of this—the flattening of my interest, the mood swings, and the strange experiences—has been building and sticking around for a little over two years, ever since my husband started traveling more and the kids became more independent.\n- psychotic_symptoms_duration: The whispers and the fixed idea that my family is somehow observing or testing me have been happening on and off for roughly the last year and a half.\n- manic_episode_duration: Those wired, fast‑talking, little‑sleep phases with big plans have been coming and going for close to a year and eight months now.\n- depressive_episode_duration: The heavy, empty, guilty stretches where everything feels pointless have been a problem for about seven months in total over this period.\n\nRules:\n- If the patient can specifically answer based on the profile, set matched=true, list related\n  sections/items in matched_sections, and write a short English strategy in answer_strategy\n  on what tone and content the patient should reply with.\n- If the question is not in the profile or can only be inferred, set matched=false and instruct\n  in answer_strategy to respond briefly (e.g., \"I'm not sure\", \"I haven't thought about it\").\n- If patient_turn_index is 1 (patient's first reply), ONLY focus the strategy on the chief complaint\n  (do NOT reveal other symptoms).\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"matched\": true|false, \"matched_sections\": [\"...\"], \"answer_strategy\": \"...\"}\n"

After an especially strained family conversation where she admitted that even painting and cooking no longer felt meaningful, her spouse encouraged her to see a psychiatrist to understand why she no (Death_Suicide): On my worst days I catch myself thinking it wouldn’t be so bad if I just didn’t wake up, and I’ve gone as far as picturing driving my car into a tree, but then I get scared and push I just don't seem to care about the things that used to define my days.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt this way consistently for at least two years, or is this a more recent change that has lasted for several weeks?\n\nPatient: It has actually been building and sticking around for a little over two years now, rather than just being a recent change of a few weeks. It really started back when my husband began traveling frequently for work and my kids became much more independent.\n\nDoctor: During these past two years, have you experienced periods where you felt completely back to your normal self, or has this low mood and lack of interest been present most of the time?\n\nPatient: The low, flat feelings haven't been constant and have only totaled about seven months; I actually spend much more of my time in these sped-up, high-energy phases. Even during a stretch last year when my mood felt relatively neutral, I still had the whispering and suspicious thoughts happening, so I rarely ever feel completely back to my old, normal self.\n\nDoctor: During those high-energy phases, do the whispering and suspicious thoughts persist throughout, or do they occur only during the periods when your mood is low?\n\nPatient: Yes, they definitely happen during those high-energy phases too. In fact, some of the times when I've felt the most wired and slept the least, racing around starting projects, were also when those whispers were the loudest and I felt most convinced my family was secretly testing me.\n\nDoctor: During these high-energy phases, do you experience a significantly elevated or irritable mood, or are the psychotic symptoms and the 'wired' feeling the primary features of those periods?\n\nPatient: It is definitely a mix of both. During those high-energy times, I feel really wired and almost giddy, but I also get incredibly irritable and snap over tiny things like how the dishwasher is loaded. Those sped-up phases are actually when the whispering and the feeling of being tested are at their absolute strongest.\n\nDoctor: During these high-energy and irritable phases, have you experienced other symptoms such as needing very little sleep, talking much faster than usual, or having racing thoughts that feel difficult to slow down?\n\nPatient: Yes, I definitely experience all of those. During those phases, I can get by on just three or four hours of sleep and still wake up buzzing, while my mind feels like a radio stuck between stations with thoughts moving too fast to grab. I also find myself talking incredibly fast and getting easily irritated with everyone around me."
  }

I just don't seem to care about the things that used to define my days.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt this way consistently for at least two years, or is this a more recent change that has lasted for several weeks?\n\nPatient: It has actually been building and sticking around for a little over two years now, rather than just being a recent change of a few weeks. It really started back when my husband began traveling frequently for work and my kids became much more independent.\n\nDoctor: During these past two years, have you experienced periods where you felt completely back to your normal self, or has this low mood and lack of interest been present most of the time?\n\nPatient: The low, flat feelings haven't been constant and have only totaled about seven months; I actually spend much more of my time in these sped-up, high-energy phases. Even during a stretch last year when my mood felt relatively neutral, I still had the whispering and suspicious thoughts happening, so I rarely ever feel completely back to my old, normal self.\n\nDoctor: During those high-energy phases, do the whispering and suspicious thoughts persist throughout, or do they occur only during the periods when your mood is low?\n\nPatient: Yes, they definitely happen during those high-energy phases too. In fact, some of the times when I've felt the most wired and slept the least, racing around starting projects, were also when those whispers were the loudest and I felt most convinced my family was secretly testing me.\n\nDoctor: During these high-energy phases, do you experience a significantly elevated or irritable mood, or are the psychotic symptoms and the 'wired' feeling the primary features of those periods?\n\nPatient: It is definitely a mix of both. During those high-energy times, I feel really wired and almost giddy, but I also get incredibly irritable and snap over tiny things like how the dishwasher is loaded. Those sped-up phases are actually when the whispering and the feeling of being tested are at their absolute strongest.\n\nDoctor: During these high-energy and irritable phases, have you experienced other symptoms such as needing very little sleep, talking much faster than usual, or having racing thoughts that feel difficult to slow down?\n\nPatient: Yes, I definitely experience all of those. During those phases, I can get by on just three or four hours of sleep and still wake up buzzing, while my mind feels like a radio stuck between stations with thoughts moving too fast to grab. I also find myself talking incredibly fast and getting easily irritated with everyone around me.\n\nDoctor: When you have those periods of low mood, do you also experience the whispering and suspicious thoughts, or do those specific symptoms only happen when you are in the high-energy, 'wired' phases?\n\nPatient: They are actually at their loudest during those high-energy, wired phases, but I don't typically experience them when my mood is really low. There was also a stretch last year when my mood felt completely neutral, and I still heard the whispers and felt like I was being tested. So they seem to happen during the high times and the neutral times, but not the low ones."
  }
]

    "content": "Candidate Diseases: \n[\"F25.0\", \"F25.1\", \"F20.0\"]\n\nInterview Transcript: \nDoctor: Hello, I am Dr. Smith. Could you please tell me what brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I don’t feel any interest in the things I used to love—my art, my garden, even time with my family just feels flat. My husband actually encouraged me to come see you because I've withdrawn so much from our daily life lately. I just don't seem to care about the things that used to define my days.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt this way consistently for at least two years, or is this a more recent change that has lasted for several weeks?\n\nPatient: It has actually been building and sticking around for a little over two years now, rather than just being a recent change of a few weeks. It really started back when my husband began traveling frequently for work and my kids became much more independent.\n\nDoctor: During these past two years, have you experienced periods where you felt completely back to your normal self, or has this low mood and lack of interest been present most of the time?\n\nPatient: The low, flat feelings haven't been constant and have only totaled about seven months; I actually spend much more of my time in these sped-up, high-energy phases. Even during a stretch last year when my mood felt relatively neutral, I still had the whispering and suspicious thoughts happening, so I rarely ever feel completely back to my old, normal self.\n\nDoctor: During those high-energy phases, do the whispering and suspicious thoughts persist throughout, or do they occur only during the periods when your mood is low?\n\nPatient: Yes, they definitely happen during those high-energy phases too. In fact, some of the times when I've felt the most wired and slept the least, racing around starting projects, were also when those whispers were the loudest and I felt most convinced my family was secretly testing me.\n\nDoctor: During these high-energy phases, do you experience a significantly elevated or irritable mood, or are the psychotic symptoms and the 'wired' feeling the primary features of those periods?\n\nPatient: It is definitely a mix of both. During those high-energy times, I feel really wired and almost giddy, but I also get incredibly irritable and snap over tiny things like how the dishwasher is loaded. Those sped-up phases are actually when the whispering and the feeling of being tested are at their absolute strongest.\n\nDoctor: During these high-energy and irritable phases, have you experienced other symptoms such as needing very little sleep, talking much faster than usual, or having racing thoughts that feel difficult to slow down?\n\nPatient: Yes, I definitely experience all of those. During those phases, I can get by on just three or four hours of sleep and still wake up buzzing, while my mind feels like a radio stuck between stations with thoughts moving too fast to grab. I also find myself talking incredibly fast and getting easily irritated with everyone around me.\n\nDoctor: When you have those periods of low mood, do you also experience the whispering and suspicious thoughts, or do those specific symptoms only happen when you are in the high-energy, 'wired' phases?\n\nPatient: They are actually at their loudest during those high-energy, wired phases, but I don't typically experience them when my mood is really low. There was also a stretch last year when my mood felt completely neutral, and I still heard the whispers and felt like I was being tested. So they seem to happen during the high times and the neutral times, but not the low ones.\n\nDoctor: To clarify the duration of your experiences, do you ever have these whispering or suspicious thoughts when you are not currently in a high-energy phase or a low-mood phase, and if so, for how long have they persisted in total?\n\nPatient: Yes, I definitely experience them during neutral times; last year there was a stretch where I felt neither high nor low, but I still heard the whispers and felt like I was being tested. In total, these whispering and suspicious thoughts have been happening on and off for about a year and a half now."

I also find myself talking incredibly fast and getting easily irritated with everyone around me.\n\nDoctor: When you have those periods of low mood, do you also experience the whispering and suspicious thoughts, or do those specific symptoms only happen when you are in the high-energy, 'wired' phases?\n\nPatient: They are actually at their loudest during those high-energy, wired phases, but I don't typically experience them when my mood is really low. There was also a stretch last year when my mood felt completely neutral, and I still heard the whispers and felt like I was being tested. So they seem to happen during the high times and the neutral times, but not the low ones.\n\nDoctor: To clarify the duration of your experiences, do you ever have these whispering or suspicious thoughts when you are not currently in a high-energy phase or a low-mood phase, and if so, for how long have they persisted in total?\n\nPatient: Yes, I definitely experience them during neutral times; last year there was a stretch where I felt neither high nor low, but I still heard the whispers and felt like I was being tested. In total, these whispering and suspicious thoughts have been happening on and off for about a year and a half now.\n\nDoctor: During that year and a half of hearing voices and feeling suspicious, were there any periods of at least two weeks where you experienced those symptoms without any significant mood swings at all?\n\nPatient: Yes, there was a stretch last year for a few weeks where my mood felt completely neutral—neither high nor low. Even though my mood was stable, I still heard the whispers at night and felt convinced that my husband's trips were part of a test on me."
  }

I just don't seem to care about the things that used to define my days.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt this way consistently for at least two years, or is this a more recent change that has lasted for several weeks?\n\nPatient: It has actually been building and sticking around for a little over two years now, rather than just being a recent change of a few weeks. It really started back when my husband began traveling frequently for work and my kids became much more independent.\n\nDoctor: During these past two years, have you experienced periods where you felt completely back to your normal self, or has this low mood and lack of interest been present most of the time?\n\nPatient: The low, flat feelings haven't been constant and have only totaled about seven months; I actually spend much more of my time in these sped-up, high-energy phases. Even during a stretch last year when my mood felt relatively neutral, I still had the whispering and suspicious thoughts happening, so I rarely ever feel completely back to my old, normal self.\n\nDoctor: During those high-energy phases, do the whispering and suspicious thoughts persist throughout, or do they occur only during the periods when your mood is low?\n\nPatient: Yes, they definitely happen during those high-energy phases too. In fact, some of the times when I've felt the most wired and slept the least, racing around starting projects, were also when those whispers were the loudest and I felt most convinced my family was secretly testing me.\n\nDoctor: During these high-energy phases, do you experience a significantly elevated or irritable mood, or are the psychotic symptoms and the 'wired' feeling the primary features of those periods?\n\nPatient: It is definitely a mix of both. During those high-energy times, I feel really wired and almost giddy, but I also get incredibly irritable and snap over tiny things like how the dishwasher is loaded. Those sped-up phases are actually when the whispering and the feeling of being tested are at their absolute strongest.\n\nDoctor: During these high-energy and irritable phases, have you experienced other symptoms such as needing very little sleep, talking much faster than usual, or having racing thoughts that feel difficult to slow down?\n\nPatient: Yes, I definitely experience all of those. During those phases, I can get by on just three or four hours of sleep and still wake up buzzing, while my mind feels like a radio stuck between stations with thoughts moving too fast to grab. I also find myself talking incredibly fast and getting easily irritated with everyone around me.\n\nDoctor: When you have those periods of low mood, do you also experience the whispering and suspicious thoughts, or do those specific symptoms only happen when you are in the high-energy, 'wired' phases?\n\nPatient: They are actually at their loudest during those high-energy, wired phases, but I don't typically experience them when my mood is really low. There was also a stretch last year when my mood felt completely neutral, and I still heard the whispers and felt like I was being tested. So they seem to happen during the high times and the neutral times, but not the low ones.\n\nDoctor: To clarify the duration of your experiences, do you ever have these whispering or suspicious thoughts when you are not currently in a high-energy phase or a low-mood phase, and if so, for how long have they persisted in total?\n\nPatient: Yes, I definitely experience them during neutral times; last year there was a stretch where I felt neither high nor low, but I still heard the whispers and felt like I was being tested. In total, these whispering and suspicious thoughts have been happening on and off for about a year and a half now.\n\nDoctor: During that year and a half of hearing voices and feeling suspicious, were there any periods of at least two weeks where you experienced those symptoms without any significant mood swings at all?\n\nPatient: Yes, there was a stretch last year for a few weeks where my mood felt completely neutral—neither high nor low. Even though my mood was stable, I still heard the whispers at night and felt convinced that my husband's trips were part of a test on me.\n\nDoctor: During that period of several weeks where your mood was neutral, were the whispering and suspicious thoughts the only symptoms you experienced, or did you also have other changes in your behavior or thinking?\n\nPatient: Yes, during those neutral weeks, the nighttime whispering and the strong feeling that my husband's trips were a test were really the main things happening. I didn't experience any of the extreme high energy, racing thoughts, or deep sadness during that time. My mood just felt mostly flat and normal, aside from those unsettling experiences."

Her family noticed that she stopped participating in shared activities, let household tasks lapse despite being at home, and spent hours isolated in the rose garden or at her laptop without finishing projects she had started, which led to growing tension and arguments with her spouse about her apparent loss of motivation and engagement. After an especially strained family conversation where she admitted that even painting and cooking no longer felt meaningful, her spouse encouraged her to see a psychiatrist to understand why she no longer cared about the activities and relationships that once defined her days.\n- Chief Complaint: “I don’t feel any interest in the things I used to love—my art, my garden, even time with my family just feels flat.”\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S019 (Delusions): I keep coming back to this idea that my husband’s work trips and the kids pulling away aren’t normal changes, but part of a plan to push me out of the family so they can watch how I react, and no matter how many times they tell me that’s not true, it still feels like there’s some test going on that I’m failing.\n- I just don't seem to care about the things that used to define my days.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt this way consistently for at least two years, or is this a more recent change that has lasted for several weeks?\n\nPatient: It has actually been building and sticking around for a little over two years now, rather than just being a recent change of a few weeks. It really started back when my husband began traveling frequently for work and my kids became much more independent.\n\nDoctor: During these past two years, have you experienced periods where you felt completely back to your normal self, or has this low mood and lack of interest been present most of the time?\n\nPatient: The low, flat feelings haven't been constant and have only totaled about seven months; I actually spend much more of my time in these sped-up, high-energy phases. Even during a stretch last year when my mood felt relatively neutral, I still had the whispering and suspicious thoughts happening, so I rarely ever feel completely back to my old, normal self.\n\nDoctor: During those high-energy phases, do the whispering and suspicious thoughts persist throughout, or do they occur only during the periods when your mood is low?\n\nPatient: Yes, they definitely happen during those high-energy phases too. In fact, some of the times when I've felt the most wired and slept the least, racing around starting projects, were also when those whispers were the loudest and I felt most convinced my family was secretly testing me.\n\nDoctor: During these high-energy phases, do you experience a significantly elevated or irritable mood, or are the psychotic symptoms and the 'wired' feeling the primary features of those periods?\n\nPatient: It is definitely a mix of both. During those high-energy times, I feel really wired and almost giddy, but I also get incredibly irritable and snap over tiny things like how the dishwasher is loaded. Those sped-up phases are actually when the whispering and the feeling of being tested are at their absolute strongest.\n\nDoctor: During these high-energy and irritable phases, have you experienced other symptoms such as needing very little sleep, talking much faster than usual, or having racing thoughts that feel difficult to slow down?\n\nPatient: Yes, I definitely experience all of those. During those phases, I can get by on just three or four hours of sleep and still wake up buzzing, while my mind feels like a radio stuck between stations with thoughts moving too fast to grab. I also find myself talking incredibly fast and getting easily irritated with everyone around me.\n\nDoctor: When you have those periods of low mood, do you also experience the whispering and suspicious thoughts, or do those specific symptoms only happen when you are in the high-energy, 'wired' phases?\n\nPatient: They are actually at their loudest during those high-energy, wired phases, but I don't typically experience them when my mood is really low. There was also a stretch last year when my mood felt completely neutral, and I still heard the whispers and felt like I was being tested. So they seem to happen during the high times and the neutral times, but not the low ones.\n\nDoctor: To clarify the duration of your experiences, do you ever have these whispering or suspicious thoughts when you are not currently in a high-energy phase or a low-mood phase, and if so, for how long have they persisted in total?\n\nPatient: Yes, I definitely experience them during neutral times; last year there was a stretch where I felt neither high nor low, but I still heard the whispers and felt like I was being tested. In total, these whispering and suspicious thoughts have been happening on and off for about a year and a half now.\n\nDoctor: During that year and a half of hearing voices and feeling suspicious, were there any periods of at least two weeks where you experienced those symptoms without any significant mood swings at all?\n\nPatient: Yes, there was a stretch last year for a few weeks where my mood felt completely neutral—neither high nor low. Even though my mood was stable, I still heard the whispers at night and felt convinced that my husband's trips were part of a test on me.\n\nDoctor: During that period of several weeks where your mood was neutral, were the whispering and suspicious thoughts the only symptoms you experienced, or did you also have other changes in your behavior or thinking?\n\nPatient: Yes, during those neutral weeks, the nighttime whispering and the strong feeling that my husband's trips were a test were really the main things happening. I didn't experience any of the extreme high energy, racing thoughts, or deep sadness during that time. My mood just felt mostly flat and normal, aside from those unsettling experiences.\n\nDoctor: During those weeks when your mood was neutral and you were experiencing the whispering and suspicious thoughts, did you also notice any other symptoms like social withdrawal, lack of motivation, or a decline in your ability to take care of daily tasks?"
  }

Subtypes: Failure to Control, Intrusive Worry, Chaining of Worry\n[S058] Restlessness_Tension: Restlessness, or feeling keyed up or on edge.  Subtypes: Feeling Keyed Up, Physical Tension, Nervousness\n[S059] Concentration_Difficulty: Difficulty concentrating or mind going blank.  Subtypes: Interference by Worry, Inability to Sustain Attention, Mind Going Blank\n[S060] Irritability: A state of reacting sensitively or sharply to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Low Frustration Tolerance, Snappiness, Exhausted Patience\n[S061] Muscle_Tension: A state where muscles are continuously tense, stiff, and accompanied by discomfort.  Subtypes: Neck and Shoulder Tension, Jaw Tension, Generalized Muscle Aches, Chronic Stiffness\n[S062] Specific_Phobic_Fear: Marked fear or anxiety about a specific object or situation that is out of proportion to the actual danger. Subtypes: Anticipatory Anxiety, Immediate Panic Response, Physiological Panic Symptoms, Freezing\n[S064] Phobic_Avoidance_Endure: The phobic object or situation is actively avoided or endured with intense fear or anxiety.
